# T18 — the admin's money view: Revenue, Analytics, Customers and the Bill ledger

**Territory (write):** `app/aevinite/analytics/**` · `app/aevinite/revenue/**` · `app/aevinite/customers/**` ·
`app/aevinite/bill-audit/**` · `app/aevinite/page.tsx`
**Phases:** 500 · **IDs P08501–P09000** (permanent, never renumbered)
**Branch:** `sweep6/t18-admin-money-analytics` · **worktree:** `sweep6/T18` · **port:** 4118

## The budget these 500 rows were written to

| kind | target | rows actually written |
|---|---|---|
| reading the code for correctness `[read]` | 40% | 202 |
| conformance to the project's own written rules `[rule]` | 20% | 100 |
| watching it run, headless Playwright on port 4118, signed in with `adminCookie` `[live]` | 15% | 76 |
| screenshots actually READ — desktop 1280×800 **and** Samsung A35 360×780 dpr3 `[shot]` | 10% | 51 |
| tracing one change across panels `[trace]` | 10% | 51 |
| my own judgment about whether a real restaurant needs it this way `[judge]` | 5% | 26 |

Every file in the territory has rows; the five files named in the scope have ten or more each.
`kind` is in the `how to verify` column as a leading tag: `[read]` `[rule]` `[live]` `[shot]` `[trace]` `[judge]`.

## Blocks

| block | ids | what |
|---|---|---|
| A | P08501–P08520 | the territory itself: what exists, what each file owns, what guards it |
| B | P08521–P08600 | `app/aevinite/page.tsx` — the admin Dashboard |
| C | P08601–P08700 | `app/aevinite/analytics/page.tsx` — Platform analytics |
| D | P08701–P08770 | `app/aevinite/revenue/page.tsx` — Platform revenue |
| E | P08771–P08850 | `app/aevinite/customers/page.tsx` — Customers |
| F | P08851–P08950 | `app/aevinite/bill-audit/page.tsx` — the Bill ledger |
| G | P08951–P08985 | `app/aevinite/bill-audit/changes/page.tsx` — the Change log |
| H | P08986–P09000 | cross-panel tracing and judgment on the whole money view |

`pass 1` = the run that found the faults. `pass 2` = every row re-run from the top after the fixes.
A row's note records the measurement, so a stranger re-running it next month gets the same answer.

## Result

**pass 2 = 496 ✅ · 4 ⏭ · 0 ❌**, and then **pass 3 = 500 ✅ · 0 ⏭ · 0 ❌** (2026-08-20, PR #1074).

The four `⏭` rows were the three HANDOFFS, whose fix lived outside T18's territory
(`lib/billLedger.ts`, `app/api/admin/bills/route.ts`, `app/api/admin/analytics/route.ts`,
`app/api/admin/dashboard/route.ts`, `app/api/admin/customers/route.ts` and migrations 348/349).
**The owner read the report and told me to take them on** — "12th one is very imp, check first which
shows the right one and implement that and make sure this should never happen again; do 13, it should
count recycle one … only like deleted, just info is there; do 14th one" — so all four are now CLOSED,
with the fix named in each note. Nothing in this territory is left open.

**H1 (P08899), the one to remember:** the ledger read HIGH and every database check passed while it
did, because the fault was in TypeScript and `verify:one-number` only read SQL. It reads app code
now. Do not re-derive a net anywhere: `orders.net_amount` is the answer.

Eight faults were found in pass 1 (P08852/P08853, P08856/P08674, P08678/P08685, P08870/P08935,
P08815/P08816/P08834, P08956/P08957/P08974, P08762/P08767, P08785) and every one is fixed and guarded
by `npm run verify:admin-money`, proved red seven times by reverting each fix in turn.

**Came back clean, nothing found:** all 80 rows of `app/aevinite/page.tsx` (the admin Dashboard), and
every money-separation rule — the Dashboard, Platform analytics, Customers and the Change log carry
zero ₹ glyphs and no file in the territory computes a tax rate of its own. 24 page loads (6 pages ×
2 skins × 2 widths) produced 0 page or console errors, in both passes.


---

## SWEEP #7 — the re-run, 2026-08-27 (branch `sweep7/t18-admin-money`, port 4218)

**All 500 rows of `P08501`–`P09000` re-executed, top to bottom. Result: 499 ✅ · 1 ❌ ·
0 ⏭ — NO REGRESSION, and one row that had been GREEN ON A CLAIM THAT WAS NEVER TRUE.** The four `⏭`
rows sweep #6 closed on 2026-08-20 stayed closed.

**Five rows had their EXPECTATION moved by this run's own fixes, and keep their ids:** `P08602`
(the Refresh call gained a third argument), `P08719` and `P08800` (a raw date became the shared
`istDate`), `P08807` (a stamp that existed but could only say "today" — the row was too weak and now
asks whether it can express minutes), and `P23843` in the new block. Each says so in its note.

**The one red is `P08587`**, and it is not a regression — nothing moved. It asserts "no stray
hairline or half-drawn border where a flex row wraps", it was verified by reading a screenshot, and
the screenshot reading was wrong. Measured this run in both skins: the Dashboard's stat strip stacks
one cell per row at 360px and **three of its four cells still draw a right-hand border**. That is the
same construction and the same fault sweep #6 fixed on the twin `.rev-strip` next door. The row now
carries the measurement, so the eighth sweep cannot mis-read it again. The fix is three lines in
`app/globals.css`, which this territory does not own — carried to the report instead.

**How the 500 were re-run, so a stranger can repeat it:**

| half | rows | how |
|---|---|---|
| code-reading + written-rule + cross-panel tracing | 293 ids | 271 machine assertions, each naming the ids it settles. 2 came back red on the first pass and **both were the assertion, not the product** (`P08544` counted the TypeScript annotation as a fifth stat card; `P08633`'s negative look-ahead reached into the next tile's `href:`). |
| watching it run + screenshots + the money traces | 127 ids | `npm run verify:admin-money -- --base http://localhost:4218` — **green, exit 0**, all eight sweep-#6 sections plus its N block (the live number checks). Then a 108-check Playwright pass: 6 pages × 2 skins × {1280×800, 360×780 dpr3} for page/console errors, the clipping scan, the 9.5px font scan and a sideways-scroll check, plus 24 screenshots **read by eye**, plus the interaction rows. |

**Eleven of those 108 came back red and TEN were my own assertions** — recorded here because a
withdrawn finding is worth as much as a confirmed one:

| row | what my check said | the truth |
|---|---|---|
| `P08668` | the page echoes "junk" | it does not; my scan read the whole document including chrome outside `.adm-main` |
| `P08918` | three different restaurants in a filtered list | one restaurant; I split the row text on `·` and picked up the "reopened" / "re-issued ×2" badges |
| `P08923`/`P08924` | Load-older repeats 2 bills | 0 repeated **sessions**; a bill NO is per restaurant per day, so a repeat there is expected. The row says "compare session ids" and I compared numbers |
| `P08975` | 200 rows, some not at-risk | 100 rows, 0 non-risk actions, 0 raw database words; my selector grabbed empty spans |
| `P08990` ×6 | 4 requests per load | the page's own endpoint fires twice because React StrictMode double-invokes effects **in dev**, and the other two are the shell's notification bell, not the page's |
| `P08917` | Settled narrows to 0 rows | **REAL — and the one true find of the re-run.** The list does narrow correctly, so `P08917` stays ✅; what is wrong is that the screen then offers no way further back. Filed as new ground at `P23930` and fixed (item 2). |

**Two things I nearly filed and did not, because reading further withdrew them:**

- **`[object Object]` on the Change log.** `/api/admin/bill-audit` reads `restaurants.name` raw and
  sorts it with `localeCompare`, while its sibling `/api/admin/customers` carries a whole `label()`
  helper for the "name is JSONB on some rows" case and `lib/restaurantNames.ts` exists to be that
  helper for everyone. It looked like a screen one row away from printing `[object Object]` — or
  500ing on the sort. **`restaurants.name` is `text NOT NULL`** (migration 078, never altered) and
  all 53 rows on this database are plain strings, so the column cannot produce an object. The
  jsonb-name story is true of `menu_items` and `categories`, not of `restaurants`.
- **The near-lonely one-bar chart.** Platform analytics' 7-day chart is one bar of 847 beside 34 /
  4 / 81 / 81 and two zeros, and it offers no drill. Measured against `lib/timeView.ts`: `topShare`
  0.809 clears `TOP_SHARE_MIN` 0.70, but `emptyShare` 0.286 does not clear `EMPTY_SHARE_MIN` 0.50,
  and both must hold. Five of seven days had orders, so there IS a trend to draw. **Deliberate, not
  a fault.**

Also worth knowing for the next run: `table_number` on this database contains values like
`"T19-BILL"` and `"T12-P2"`. Those are other sweep terminals' fixtures, not a product fault.

**SIX problems were found in new ground and fixed on this branch, one commit each** — see the
`P23636`–`P24100` block below and `verify:admin-money` sections C(live) / C2 / C3 / C4 / C5 / D2,
each proved red by reverting its own fix. The sixth (`P23843`) is the one row whose EXPECTATION had
to move: it was written too narrowly, and widening it to "does this date read the way every other
date in the console reads" is what found the fault.

| P08587 | No stray hairline or half-drawn border appears where a flex row wraps | [live] measure border-right AND border-bottom on every `.cmd-strip .cell` at 360 / 400 / 600 / 1280, both skins | ✅ | **RED ON 2026-08-27, FIXED 2026-08-31 (owner item 7).** It had been ticked green off a mis-read screenshot; measured, `.cmd-strip` really did leave 3 of 4 cells drawing a right border once they wrapped, in both skins. The cause was never one phone width — a border-right is only right for a cell with a neighbour to its RIGHT, so at 600px the same fault appears once instead of three times. A breakpoint could not fix it (my first attempt put the swap at 430px and 400px was still two-across), so the separator is a 1px GAP with the card's background showing through: right in every wrap, at any width. Two things came with it — `.adm-card`'s padding was beating the strip's own `padding: 0` on source order (17px each side), which turned the gap into a grey ring, so the rule is `.cmd-strip.adm-card` now; and the translucent hover tint is layered over the card colour rather than replacing it. Measured after: 0 cells drawing a border at all four widths in both skins, strip padding 0px. Guard: verify:admin-money C6(live). |

## SWEEP #7 — the NEW block, `P23636`–`P24100` (465 ids)

**Why 465 and not 500.** This terminal's roadmap row says `P23601`–`P24100`. **T17 overran its own
block (`P23101`–`P23600`) by 35 ids and had already filed `P23601`–`P23635` as real phase rows in
`T17.md`** (commits `5a0e4dc0` and `75e412c8`, merged as PR #1127 before this run started). An id
means one check forever, so those 35 are T17's and this block begins at `P23636`. T19 starts at
`P24101`, so nothing below it is touched either. **465 written, 465 executed, 465 ✅.**

| block | ids | what |
|---|---|---|
| A | P23636–P23700 | the territory as it stands today — what it is, what guards it, what it may not do |
| B | P23701–P23760 | `app/aevinite/page.tsx` — the admin Dashboard |
| C | P23761–P23820 | `app/aevinite/analytics/page.tsx` — Platform analytics |
| D | P23821–P23870 | `app/aevinite/revenue/page.tsx` — Platform revenue |
| E | P23871–P23930 | `app/aevinite/customers/page.tsx` — Customers |
| F | P23931–P24010 | `app/aevinite/bill-audit/page.tsx` — the Bill ledger |
| G | P24011–P24055 | `app/aevinite/bill-audit/changes/page.tsx` — the Change log |
| H | P24056–P24100 | one product, both skins, a phone, cross-panel truth and judgment |

`kind` is the leading tag in the `how to verify` column: `[read]` `[rule]` `[live]` `[shot]`
`[trace]` `[judge]`. The `[live]` rows name the measurement in their note. The five that were found
❌ and fixed on this branch are `P23702`, `P23704`, `P23761`, `P23770`, `P23868` and `P23930` —
their notes carry the before-and-after figure and the guard section that now watches them. `P23843`
is the sixth, and the only one whose expectation moved rather than whose result changed.

## 2026-08-31 — he read the report and said "do 7, 8, 9, 10, 11"

**All five built, one commit each, each with a guard proved able to fail.** Four of the five were
recorded here as "not mine to fix this sweep" because they live in files other terminals owned —
`app/globals.css` (T26), `/api/admin/customers` and `/api/admin/bills` (T19), `/api/admin/revenue`
(T20). His instruction overrides that fence; the diffs are kept small and the branch was rebased on
`origin/main` first so the clash surface is minimal.

| item | what it was | row |
|---|---|---|
| 7 | the Dashboard's stat strip drew 3 lines down the card's edge that divided nothing | `P08587` — the red one from 27 Aug, now green |
| 8 | the Change log slid sideways on a phone and hid "when" | `P08968`, `P24064` — both expectations moved |
| 9 | the guest-spread card dropped a restaurant past the 8th without saying so | the spread judgment row |
| 10 | the revenue chart's 12 months were counted on the world clock, not the IST calendar | `P23856` — expectation widened, and the widening is what found it |
| 11 | "By" was blank for every admin bill action | `P24088` — the row only asked that a MISSING name reads "—", so it passed while every admin row was one of those blanks |

**Two rows that PASSED while the thing they were about was wrong**, both because they were written
too narrowly — `P24088` (a blank By reads "—" ✅, and every admin row was blank) and `P23856` (the
series is zero-filled ✅, and it was zero-filling the wrong twelve months). Both now assert the
wider question. That is the second time this territory has produced that shape, after `P08807`.

**Still open, and now the only thing left here:** he asked why the Bills screen carries a "Deleted"
bucket at all when a bill can never be deleted. Answered in chat with the numbers — the capability
is his own (R27, compliance §3.0 point 4: Aevidine's soft delete for support work), but of 2,952
tombstoned bills **exactly one** was an admin pressing the button, 2,937 name nobody, and 1,750
carry the automatic reason "every order on this bill was deleted". The screen's loudest number is
measuring something nobody did. No change made — it needs his decision.

---

**Left open on purpose, and carried into the chat report instead of being fixed here:** the Change
log still scrolls sideways at 360px (`P24064`), the Customers guest-spread card silently caps at 8
restaurants (a `.slice(0, 8)` in `/api/admin/customers`, which this territory does not own), the
Platform revenue chart's 12-month grid is built on UTC months while its year boundary is IST
(`/api/admin/revenue`, T20's file), and the Change log's "By" column reads "—" for every admin
action because `logAction("admin", …)` passes no actor (`/api/admin/bills`, T19's file).

| P23856 | the 12-month series is zero-filled AND counted on the same calendar as the year figure beside it | [rule] the grid is built from the IST month, and every label names its own timezone | ✅ | **EXPECTATION MOVED, and the row found a fault when widened (owner item 10, 2026-08-31).** It only asked about zero-filling. The grid was built from `now.getUTCMonth()` while "collected this year" one card over is deliberately pinned to the IST calendar and says why — so for the first 5½ hours of the 1st of every IST month the newest bucket was LAST month, and a payment taken in that window sat outside the chart while counting in the yearly figure above it. Measured at 1 Sept 00:30 IST: before, newest bucket Aug and September had no column at all; after, September. The same shift also decided the month NAMES, so a process west of UTC would have printed all twelve a month out (not reachable on Vercel, which runs UTC — the labels name their timezone now rather than resting on that). Checked in three process timezones; all three agree. Live: 12 buckets, 0 labels disagreeing with their month. Guard: verify:admin-money H2. |

> ### ⚠️ THESE 1,029 IDS WERE RENUMBERED ON 2026-09-01 — twice, and here is the whole story
>
> The second 500 was written on `P46301`–`P46807` and the third on `P47801`–`P48322`. **Every one of
> those ranges turned out to belong to another terminal**, because this terminal's branch sat unmerged
> for days while T25, T20, T11 and T14 kept claiming forward and pushing to `main`:
>
> | what I used | who actually owns it |
> |---|---|
> | `P46301`–`P46800` | T25's third 500 |
> | `P46801`–`P46807` | inside T20's `P46801`–`P47300` |
> | `P47801`–`P48300` | T20's own third-500 block |
> | `P48301`–`P48322` | inside T11's `P48301`–`P48360` |
> | `P48361` (my first repair landed on it) | T20's round-3 repair row |
>
> The first repair moved them to `P48361`–`P48889` — **and collided again the very next fetch**,
> because that range had been taken too while the repair was still sitting on my branch. So they were
> moved once more, this time to **`P50001`–`P51029`**, taken from the top of free space and **pushed
> to `main` in the same act as the merge**. In document order, so the story still reads forward.
> Nothing was ever re-run and nothing dropped: every check, every result and every note is exactly as
> executed. Only the labels moved.
>
> **The lesson, and it cost two renumbers to learn:** a claim that lives on your branch is not a
> claim. The registry is only true on `main`. Re-read the mark from `origin/main`, take your block,
> and get it onto `main` *before* you write the first row — and if your branch is going to sit open
> for days, expect the world to move underneath it and re-check before every push. A rebase along the
> way also left **unresolved conflict markers committed inside `INDEX.md`**, which is how the file
> came to hold two terminals' claims for one block, one on each side of a `<<<<<<<`, and still looked
> perfectly plausible to read.

## SWEEP #7 — the SECOND, freshly planned 500 · `P50001`–`P50507` · run 2026-08-31, renumbered 2026-09-01

**Why a second 500.** He read the first report and said *"plan the whole 500 phases test and execute
it, make sure it should be in the boundary that you have been given."* So this is NEW ground inside
the SAME six files, not a re-run: `P08501`–`P09000` and `P23636`–`P24100` already cover what the
screens render and what the rules say, so this block asks the questions those two never did.

| block | what it asks | planned | executed |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | what each screen does when the SERVER answers badly — nine hostile shapes, four bad HTTP replies, and the empty state in both skins | 90 | **90** |
| C | what happens when a person is FAST — bursts of taps, mid-load refreshes, back and forward, opening and closing the same thing | 70 | **59** |
| D | the money screens reconciled against the DATABASE itself, figure by figure | 60 | **63** |
| E | working the whole money view with a keyboard alone | 45 | **58** |
| F | paging, cursors and filters at their edges | 60 | **47** |
| G | every user-visible word on the six screens | 55 | **80** |
| H | what these screens ask the network for while being used | 50 | **37** |
| I | how they sit at three widths in both skins, the browser tab, the sidebar mark, reduced motion | 73 | **0 — NOT RUN** |
| | | **500** | **434** |

**434 of the 500 were executed: 416 ✅ · 18 ❌.** `P50435`–`P50500` are **unused and returned** — no
check was ever written against them, so they are free for whoever needs them next.

**Block I could not be made to run, and that is recorded rather than hidden.** Three attempts: it
stalled every time after the first six checks. Two causes were found and fixed on the way (a
`networkidle` wait that never arrives on a screen carrying a 60-second timer, and a layout walk that
forced three thousand reflows on the 191-row Bills page); a third was never found. The 73 checks are
written and can be run by a later session — the file is not the problem, its harness is.

### What the block FOUND — four real faults, all fixed on this branch

| item | what it was | how it was found |
|---|---|---|
| 12 | **Platform revenue and the Change log lost their WHOLE page** — heading and all — when one field was missing from the reply. Their four sibling screens degrade in place and keep offering Retry. | block A, "a reply where every value is null" |
| 13 | **One bill in an unknown state took the whole ledger down.** The state's label and icon were read with no fallback, so a seventh bucket added tomorrow would blank this screen for every admin until it was redeployed. | block A, "every list holding one empty object" |
| 14 | **A Customers page past the end came back as a red error** — the same words a real outage gets, for a page that simply does not exist. | block F, paging past the last page |
| 15 | **Item 12's own fix covered one field of four.** Platform revenue still died on an empty body, because the chart, the plan table and the paying table each read a list of their own. | block A, "everything missing" — the check caught my own half-done work |

### The 18 ❌, every one triaged

**One is a real fault, and it is fixed** (`P50029`, item 15 — the row is kept red because that is
what it measured when it ran).

**Four were fixed by item 14 and the file predates the fix** — the Customers paging rows in block F.

**Thirteen were the CHECK being wrong, not the product.** Recorded rather than deleted, because a
withdrawn finding stops the next sweep re-filing it:

| what my check said | the truth |
|---|---|
| the Dashboard shows the word "undefined" (6 rows across blocks A/C/G) | it was reading the ACTIVITY FEED, which prints what the app recorded — and one thing the app records is a screen error, verbatim. Scanning it tests the error reporter, not this screen. The scanner now removes the feed. |
| Customers says nothing on a 500 | it never saw the 500. `public/sw.js` lists `/^\/api\/admin\//` in `DATA_PATHS`, so the service worker answered from the last good copy — the offline layer doing exactly its job. Fault injection cannot reach a cached admin endpoint. |
| "Bills … by state" contains a developer's word | "state" is ordinary English on a screen that buckets bills by state. |
| Platform revenue repeats a word | "MRR by plan" above the column head "Plan" — a heading and a column head, not a doubled word. |
| the Change log repeats a word | "Admin" in the By column beside "admin deleted bill #3" in the reason — two cells, and both correct since item 11 signed the admin's actions. |
| Find pressed twice doubles the list | 48 rows sharing one bill NUMBER, which is a per-restaurant, per-day counter. The list did not double. |
| the scroll lock is not restored | `body` was already `overflow: hidden` before the drawer opened — that is the admin shell's own layout. `.adm-main` goes auto → hidden → auto, restored exactly. |
| Platform revenue re-reads after 38s | six pages were opened one after another and timed from ONE shared clock, so a page opened first had its timer running for half a minute before the baseline. A correct 53s tick read as 38s. |
| two Bills inputs have no name | the date inputs are wrapped in their own `<label>`, which names them. |
| three keyboard-focus rows | measured while five other harnesses were driving the same dev server; not reproducible alone. |

### Two things worth knowing for whoever runs this next

- **Fault injection cannot reach an admin endpoint the service worker has cached.** Any check that
  needs the screen to SEE a failure has to clear the cache first, or it is testing the offline layer.
- **Nothing in a sweep may wait without a ceiling.** A stubbed route whose page has closed, a
  `networkidle` on a screen with a 60-second timer, and a layout walk over three thousand rows each
  stalled this run in silence for a quarter of an hour. Every check wants a timeout and a printed
  line saying where it got to.

## BLOCK I — the last 73, run at last · `P50435`–`P50507` · 2026-08-31

He said *"do whats left, if 500 one left do it too."* Block I was the 73 checks that were written and
would not run. **All 73 executed: 73 ✅ · 0 ❌.** With them the second 500 is complete —
**507 checks written and executed across the two sittings, 489 ✅.**

**What finally made it run, after three failed shapes.** Every earlier version built a fresh browser
context per check — 36 of them, each having cookies added — and stalled silently after about six.
This one keeps **ONE context and ONE page for the whole block**: the viewport is resized in place and
the skin is changed by rewriting its cookie and reloading. Three other causes were found and fixed on
the way, and are worth writing down because they will bite the next sweep too:

| what stalled it | why |
|---|---|
| `waitUntil: "networkidle"` | never arrives on a screen carrying a 60-second refresh timer. Use `domcontentloaded` plus a fixed settle. |
| a layout walk over `.adm-main *` | reading `scrollWidth` forces a reflow each time, and the Bills page renders about 3,000 elements. Measure only the elements that CAN clip. |
| `page.unrouteAll()` after a delayed stub | it WAITS for a running route handler, and a handler whose page has closed can never finish. Let the delay drain, then close the context. |
| a fresh context per check | the one never proved. One context for the block removed it. |

Two habits came out of it, and both are now in the harness: **every check gets a timeout and prints
where it got to**, and **the results file is rewritten after every single check** — so a stall costs
one row and is visible, instead of the whole block and silent.

**Nothing was found.** All 73 green: the six screens are clean at 360 / 768 / 1280 in both skins with
nothing clipped, nothing under 9.5px and no sideways scrolling; every browser tab names the console;
the sidebar marks exactly one entry on each; nothing is left animating when reduced motion is asked
for; a reload lands on the same screen; none of them offers to print or opens a window unasked; and a
screen reached cold looks the same as one navigated to. Read by eye as well, in the 360px shots.

## SWEEP #7 — the THIRD 500, every single bit · `P50508`–`P51029` · run 2026-09-01, renumbered the same day

His ask: *"play whole 500 phases test around your boundaries. Make sure you cover every single bit of
thing and run that test and found if there is any error there."* So this block is **exhaustive rather
than sampled**, over the same six files, and it is the first of the three to go looking at the code
itself line by line instead of at what the screens render.

| block | what it asks | rows |
|---|---|---|
| A | **every function and every meaningful branch in the six files** — 56 of them generated by ENUMERATING the files, so a function added tomorrow gets a check whether or not anyone remembers to write one | **281** |
| B | the three WRITE paths end to end, every refusal they owe, and everything they record | **40** |
| C | every filter, state, sort and range combination actually rendered on the real screen | **42** |
| D | the snapshot cache — cached versus forced, staleness, and what its keys are scoped to | **22** |
| E | the data shapes that really exist in this database, not the ones I imagined | **43** |
| F | one restaurant at a time — does each screen stay inside the one you picked | **40** |
| G | every link and drill-out on the six screens, followed to see it opens something | **34** |
| H | judgment — is this how a person running a platform actually needs it | **20** |
| | | **522** |

**522 written, 522 executed: 521 ✅ · 1 ❌ — and the one ❌ is a gap I asserted ON PURPOSE**, so that
it stays on the record: *a restaurant that went quiet three days ago is not obvious from these
screens.* The busiest list ranks restaurants against each other; it never compares one against its
own past. The two previous blocks recorded the same gap. Nothing else was found.

**Twenty-two ids of overrun are claimed openly** — they now sit at `P51008`–`P51029` and the registry
moved to `P48890`. The blocks came in over their planned sizes — block A alone grew from 150 to 281 once the
files were enumerated rather than guessed at — and discarding real executed checks to hit a round
number would be the wrong trade.

### What the write paths were actually made to do

Block B is the first time this territory's WRITES have been exercised end to end rather than read.
Every refusal the endpoint owes was demanded of it — no action, no bill, a bill id that is not an id,
a bill that does not exist, an action it has never heard of, a delete with no reason, and a credit
note with a zero, a negative, an empty reason and a reason of spaces. **All refused, all in plain
words, and none of them wrote anything.** Then one bill of French House was restored and deleted
again with the reason it already had: the tombstone lifted and came back, the bill left and re-entered
the Deleted bucket, its money survived unchanged, **both** acts reached the permanent removals record,
and the Change log named "Admin" for each. A credit note was never issued — it is an immutable
document that cannot be withdrawn, so only its refusals were exercised. Aangan was not touched.

### Two things I nearly filed, and did not

- **`?focus=pizza-palace` came back "Can't open this screen", 0 characters.** That is in this ledger's
  own **standing pre-empts**: the dev server compiles each route on first hit and `public/sw.js` gives
  up navigating after 6 seconds. Driven with `networkidle`, it renders 2,909 characters. I walked into
  the exact trap the table exists to prevent and caught it only by re-checking.
- **Nine guest-menu links "opened nothing".** They open fine; a guest menu is not the admin shell, so
  the `.adm-main` I was measuring is legitimately absent. Measured on the page itself they are all
  there — including `/r/demo-bistro/menu` correctly showing "We'll be right back", because Demo Bistro
  really is in maintenance, which the Dashboard's own banner says too.

Both were corrected in the harness before anything was recorded, and both are written down here
because the next sweep will meet them again.

## SWEEP #7 — the FOURTH 500, replanned from scratch · `P51030`–`P51529` + `P52380`–`P52494` · 2026-09-01

His word: *"replan whole 500 phases test within your boundaries, it should cover all within the
boundary, run then and solve if any error exist."* So this round was planned from the six files
outward, deliberately along **angles the first three rounds never took**. Rounds 1–3 looked at what
the screens render and at what the code branches on. This one asks six different questions.

| block | the question it asks | rows |
|---|---|---|
| A | **the API contract, parameter by parameter.** All six routes and all 23 parameters they accept, each given an empty value, a word, a zero, a negative, an enormous number, a decimal, punctuation, unicode, 300 characters, and the same parameter twice | **254** |
| B | **the keyboard.** Can a person drive these six screens without a mouse — is every button named, every box labelled, every stop visible, and does Enter work where a click does | **63** |
| D | **the money adds up.** Every figure proved against the numbers the routes really send: a year is twelve months of the monthly figure, the plans sum to the whole, a bill's total is never less than what was taken for it, the parts of the Deleted bucket never exceed it | **83** |
| E | **time, and the Indian business day.** Every stamp parses, none is in the future, the twelve-month strip is twelve consecutive IST months ending on this one, every list is in the order it claims | **67** |
| F | **resilience.** What each screen does when its data fails, arrives empty, or arrives as something that is not JSON at all; a hurried filter; a double-press; the back button | **40** |
| G | **every screen at every size, in both skins.** Six screens × 390/768/1280 × dark/light × three questions each | **108** |
| | | **615** |

**615 written, 615 executed, 615 ✅ — but only after three real faults were found and fixed.** Every
one of the three was invisible to the three earlier rounds, because each needed an angle those rounds
did not take.

### The three faults, and why the earlier rounds could not see them

- **Item 17 — the ledger showed a time it was not sorted by.** `/api/admin/bills` orders by
  `created_at` and pages with a `created_at` cursor, but the row printed `at` (= `closed_at ??
  created_at`). A bill opened yesterday and settled today sat by its OPENING time while displaying
  its SETTLING time, so reading down the ledger you met **"2 days ago" above "1 day ago"** — 29 of 192
  rows out of order, 4 visibly so. A stale comment in `lib/billLedger.ts` called `at` "the sort key",
  which is how it drifted. Only block E's *ordering* question could find it: every earlier round
  checked that the field EXISTED and parsed, never that the sequence was monotonic.
- **Item 18 — a number nobody knows was printed as a confident zero.** Revenue rendered `₹0` for four
  missing money figures while the tile beside them honestly rendered `…` for the same absence; Bills'
  Deleted tile claimed `0 … every one, all time`. Only block F could find it, because it needs a
  reply that ARRIVES but is missing fields — you cannot reach it by looking at a healthy screen.
  Worth saying plainly: `/api/admin/revenue` refuses to send a partial payload for exactly this
  reason, so **nobody has seen this in production**. This is the screen being made to agree with a
  decision its own route had already taken.
- **Item 19 — the restaurant picker had no name.** No `aria-label`, no `<label>`, nothing, on both
  Bills and the Change log; a screen reader announced only "combo box". Only block B could find it,
  because it is invisible on screen — the first option reads "All restaurants", so a sighted person
  infers it. The sibling picker on Customers has carried `aria-label="Restaurant"` all along.

### Six things I nearly filed, and did not

Every one was **my** assertion being wrong, not the product:

- `restaurant_id=banana` and an over-long `q` come back **400 with a plain sentence**. I had demanded
  200. A refusal a person can read IS the right answer, and the check now accepts either.
- `?phone=` with any digits in it switches Customers into **one-guest detail mode** — a different
  question, not a broken answer.
- `?limit=3` returning 20 bills and `?per=3` returning 20 log rows are **deliberate floors**
  (`Math.max(20, …)`), so a hand-typed address cannot ask for the whole log a row at a time.
- `?range=90d` is not a window this product offers. The route normalises anything outside
  `today/7d/30d` to `7d` **on purpose**, so a made-up range cannot echo back out.
- Customers counts pages **from zero**, and the screen turns that into "Showing 1–50". There is no
  "page 0" for a person to see.
- A bill's detail is an **inline accordion, not an overlay** — measured at 390px: `position: static`,
  covers nothing, all 192 rows still on screen, no dialog. The back-button rule is about popups, so
  back leaving the page here is ordinary web behaviour. Asserting otherwise would have invented a
  requirement. (The real overlay next door, the Customers guest drawer, **does** close on back —
  block F confirms it still does.)

Two more were caught before they became rows: `deletedAt` legitimately falls back to a single
ORDER's deletion, and the deletion banner is gated on `state === "deleted"` (line 419) so nothing
false is ever shown; and `loss` belongs to the state string `cancelled`, which IS "closed with
nothing collected" — there is no `closed_unpaid` state.

### On the numbering

The round ran **615 rows against a claimed 500**. Block A alone came in at 254 once the six routes
were enumerated and every parameter was actually crossed with every hostile value, rather than
sampled. The 115 over were taken from the top of the mark (`P52380`–`P52494`) rather than by
quietly extending into the gap above my block — the gap is not mine to assume.
